市場焦點
美國
• 10月S&P Global綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖較前月回落，但仍處高檔區間；服務業動
能略為放緩，製造業PMI則升至56.0，創近4年半新高。新訂單增速加快，顯示企業需求、投
資及庫存回補動能仍具韌性，然而企業售價與投入成本同步上升，反映需求改善、供應鏈延
遲及成本轉嫁效應持續存在，通膨降溫進程仍具不確定性。整體而言，市場關注焦點轉向通
膨與利率走勢，高利率環境恐維持較長時間，進而推高股債資產評價波動。
中國
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期，在出口與科技製造業帶動下，
短期成長可望維持穩定，但若內需未能有效改善，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散，建議聚焦在
半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈，及高端製造相關產業，至於消費、房地產及傳統內需類股，

仍需觀察後續刺激政策與消費信心回升情況。
法國
政局動盪
• 法國金融市場因各地抗議活動持續擴大，面臨股債雙跌的局面。市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支
出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮。法國10年期公債殖利率一度突破5%，法德利差亦擴
大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標仍處於歷史相對低檔，遠低於歐
元區主權債務危機期間，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務危機的風險仍相對有限。

美國PMI維持強勁擴張，經濟成長續航但通膨壓力再升溫
• 企業需求強勁支撐美國景氣維持高檔擴張：美國10月綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖略低於前月，但仍處於近五年來
的高位區間，顯示企業活動持續高速擴張；其中服務業PMI為57.9、製造業PMI升至56.0並創53個月新高，新訂單增速
更創逾4年半最佳表現，反映內需穩健、出口需求改善與企業補庫存效應同步發酵，美國第四季經濟動能仍具韌性。
• 成本上升與供應瓶頸推升通膨風險：企業售價漲幅創2022年8月以來最高，能源、運輸、金屬原料及薪資成本同步上揚，
加上供應鏈交期延長與訂單積壓擴大，使企業得以持續轉嫁成本，暗示未來數月消費者通膨可能再度偏離聯準會目標區
間，市場對利率維持較高水準的預期恐因此升溫。
• 後市觀察：景氣韌性有利獲利成長，但通膨可能推升殖利率、壓抑高評價資產。投資配置建議優先布局美國大型科技股、

美國金融股及工業資本支出受惠族群，可透過NASDAQ 100 ETF、S&P 500 ETF及美國銀行股ETF參與企業獲利成長；
固定收益則以短天期投資級債為主，以降低利率波動風險。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.24
S&P Global PMI 走勢

海外資金流入美國市場，支撐美國資產吸引力
• 海外資金持續流入美國：美國財政部8月TIC資料顯示，
整體資金淨流入1,998億美元，其中民間部門淨流入
1,785億美元，為主要資金來源；外資淨買進美國長
期證券3,007億美元，遠高於美國投資人加碼海外資
產216億美元，反映全球資金在經濟成長與流動性考
量下，對美國金融市場仍維持高度的配置需求。
• 全球資金偏好美國資產：外資8月淨買入美國股票
1,319億美元，高於美國公債與公司債，顯示海外投
資人仍看好美國企業獲利與經濟基本面，雖然短期國
庫券持有量下降，部分跨境美元資金亦出現波動，但
以10月近況來看長天期公債標售仍吸引境外央行、
保險資金及大型資產管理機構參與，反映高殖利率水
準對長期資金仍具吸引力。在經濟成長維持韌性與企

業獲利支撐下，美國資產仍為全球資金配置的重要核
心市場。
• 市場看法：海外資金流入有助反映美國資產吸引力，
惟後續仍須觀察通膨、Fed政策及企業獲利表現。投
資布局建議可以美國投資等級債、美國中短天期公債
及大型AI基礎建設受惠族群為核心配置，兼顧收益、
防禦及長期成長潛力。

顧
歷史數據顯示全球股市突破歷史新高後，後續上漲機率高
• 很多人直覺以為見高就是頂，但過去20年的統計資料顯示，全球股市在創下新高後，多數時間選擇繼續向上走。
• 勝率顯著偏高：全球股市(MSCI全球指數)創新高後持有一年，獲得正報酬的機率高達62%，三個月後的正報酬機率更達
71%。美股與台股表現最亮眼，標普500指數創高後一年上漲機率為76%，那斯達克為79%；而台灣股市表現更為驚人，創
高後三個月內上漲的機率高達82%。
• 下檔拉回風險有限：在創高後短期(一個月內)修正幅度其實相當溫和，全球多數主要市場的回徹幅度中位數僅1%～2%左右。
• 突破前高優於連續創新高：若市場在歷經至少一個月以上的整理後突破前高，其未來一年的平均報酬率高達8.4%，遠高於
連續每天創新高的3.9%。這代表市場經適度整理後的突破，具備更健康的上漲動能。

創新高後的平均遠期報酬率
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.22

顧
市場寬度收窄，由少數巨頭獨撐
• 當前的市場特徵由少數巨頭帶頭衝：科技股權重較高的指數(如那斯達克、台股、韓股)，長期以來都有少數權重股拉動指數
的現象。自2024年以來，全球13個主要市場中有10個市場在創新高時，參與上漲的股票比例明顯下降。已開發市場創新高
時高於200日均線的股票比例從過去75.5%降至65.1%，新興市場更降至61.3%。這意味著指數看似風光，但多數個股並未
同步創高，當漲勢過度集中於少數大型科技巨頭時，整體市場的抗風險能力會相對減弱。
• 小結與投資建議：歷史經驗顯示，創新高本身是強勢市場的特徵，後續繼續走揚的機率遠大於立刻崩盤。然而，鑑於目前市
場寬度收窄、權重高度集中於科技股的現狀，盲目追高個股的風險已顯著增加。投資建議可以利用ETF掌握大盤趨勢，由於

指數上漲主要由龍頭股驅動，透過市值型ETF(如追蹤標普500ETF)能直接享受龍頭巨頭帶來的指數創高紅利，避免選錯個股
而賺了指數、賠了價差。此外，提防短期股市回檔，可運用定期定額分批布局或等待大盤適度拉回整理再行加碼，降低追高
風險。
創新高時不同水準的市場寬度佔比
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.22
已開發市場和新興市場創新高時的寬度

顧
法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓
• 法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓：法國各地抗議活動持續擴大，最初由學生發起，隨後因生活成本高漲而引發其
他群體響應。在財政空間有限、俄烏戰爭與美伊衝突推升能源價格的背景下，政府難以有效緩解民生成本壓力，法國金融市
場因此面臨股債雙跌的局面。
• 公債殖利率攀升，但系統性風險仍相對可控：市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮。法國10
年期公債殖利率一度突破5%，法德利差亦擴大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標（CISS）仍處於
歷史相對低檔，遠低於歐元區主權債務危機期間，顯示金融市場尚未出現廣泛的系統性壓力，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務
危機的風險仍相對有限。
G7社會動盪指數，法國高居榜首

資料來源： Beco models country，2026.10.19 資料來源： Bloomberg，2026.10.19

資料來源： Bloomberg，2026.10.15 資料來源： tradingeconomics，2026.10.21
日本貿易收支
• 日本貿易逆差擴大，進口增幅高於出口：日本2026年9月貿易逆差擴大至5,366億日圓，較去年同期的2,777億日圓增加93%，
高於市場預期的1,810億日圓，連續第五個月呈現貿易赤字。出口年增16.7%至10.97兆日圓，受惠於半導體相關電子零件、
製造設備及汽車出口成長；惟進口年增18.9%至11.51兆日圓，增幅高於出口，主要受到原油、半導體電子零件及液化天然
氣進口增加帶動。其中，原油進口年增51.1%，部分反映日本在荷姆茲海峽局勢緊張下，積極分散能源採購來源。
• 日本央行本週料維持利率不變：日本央行9月將政策利率調升25個基點至1.25%，創31年來新高。考量前幾次升息對經濟的

傳導效果仍待觀察，加上貿易逆差擴大反映進口成本壓力，多數分析師預期日本央行本週將暫停升息，以評估貨幣緊縮對經
濟與通膨的影響。

中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期：顯示中國經濟在第二季落底後呈現溫和回升，從季度表現來看，
一、二、三季增速分別為5.0%、4.3%及4.5%，反映政策支持與外需復甦開始發揮效果，全年達成官方4.5%成長目標的機率
明顯提升，第三季經濟改善的主要動能來自出口，受惠於全球企業持續擴建AI基礎設施，及科技產品需求強勁，中國高科技
產品出口表現亮眼，尤其是積體電路、伺服器、電腦零組件，及儲存設備等產品增幅顯著，高技術製造業維持雙位數成長。
同時，中國對美國及歐盟出口維持強勁，推升貿易順差走升，為支撐經濟的重要力量。然而，內需疲弱仍是當前經濟最大隱
憂，房地產調整尚未結束，居民消費與企業投資意願保守，使經濟復甦結構呈現「外熱內冷」的特徵。

• 展望後市：在出口與科技製造業帶動下，短期成長可望維持穩定，但若內需未能有效改善，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散，建
議聚焦在半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈，及高端製造相關產業，至於消費、房地產及傳統內需類股，仍需觀察後續刺激政
策與消費信心回升情況。
資料來源：開源證券，2026.10.22

彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高
• 彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高：彭博產業研究認為，中國AI產業未來將由大型科技平台主導，其中騰
訊、字節跳動與阿里巴巴憑藉龐大用戶基礎、數據資源、資金實力及完整生態系統，在AI競賽中具備明顯優勢，這些企業可將
AI廣泛應用於電商、社群、遊戲、金融與雲端業務，提升營運效率並強化競爭門檻，例如：阿里巴巴利用 AI 驅動電商平台上的
個人化產品推薦，而騰訊則運用 AI 優化短影音廣告並強化微信功能。騰訊自 2026 年 7 月起開始將混元 AI 模型整合至其電子
遊戲中，使遊戲角色及助理能更準確地回應玩家，然而，AI模型訓練與基礎建設投入龐大，獲利能力短期仍受壓抑，且須面對
監管、資料隱私及晶片供應等挑戰。此外，智譜、月之暗面、MiniMax及商湯等AI新創雖具技術創新能力，但缺乏規模效益、

穩定現金流及長期資本支持，未來獲利挑戰較大。
• 投資建議：看好中國AI長期發展者，可優先關注具資金、算力與應用場景優勢的大型平台企業，受惠程度可望高於多數AI新創
公司。

台灣9月外銷訂單再創新高，台股第四季有望先蹲後跳
• 台灣9月外銷訂單再創新高：9月外銷訂單1,142.1億美元，連兩個月破千億美元，再創單月新高；年增率高達62.4%，連續
20個月呈雙位數正成長。經濟部表示，AI強勁需求超乎預期，預估今年第四季接單還會優於第三季。展望10月表現，外銷
訂單將持續亮麗，估落在1,125億至1,159億美元，有望連三個月破千億美元。
• 台股第四季有望先蹲後跳：10月初以來，台股在盤中觸及歷史高點49,968點後面臨一波修正，主要反映在新一輪的財報公
布前，市場觀望AI投資是否存在泡沫化風險。短期來看，台股在季線附近出現支撐，不過在11月初的美國期中選舉落幕前，
台股維持區間震盪的可能性較高；中期來看，美國期中選後往往因政治不確定性下降，歷史統計股市上漲機率高，加上台灣

年底集團作帳及選舉行情，正向看待年底台股表現。
資料來源：經濟部統計處，兆豐銀行整理，2026.10.20。

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國 10 月財政赤字大幅擴張、房市買氣探底，通膨壓力仍具韌性 
經濟 
⚫ 美國 2027 財年首月財政赤字達 4,040.6 億美元，年增 42.1%。當月總收
入 3,689.4 億美元（年減 8.8%），總支出 7,730 億美元（年增 12.2%），
截至 10 月底公債總額升至 40.52 兆美元，財政壓力顯著加劇。 
⚫ 受房貸利率攀升至 7.33%影響，2026 年 10 月美國成屋銷售年率跌至 375
萬戶的 16 年新低。目前房價中位數微升至 41.67 萬美元，待售庫存增
至 159 萬戶，房市呈現「量縮、價微揚、庫存增」的態勢。考量目前就
業市場仍持續成長，一旦利率開始回落，預期將能有效釋放市場積壓的
買氣。 
⚫ 10 月美國消費者物價指數(CPI)月增 0.4%，低於上月 0.6%，惟高於市場
預期；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%持平。住房、食品及能源價格續升，其中住
房貢獻逾三成，顯示通膨壓力仍存；惟受 2025 年 10 月政府停擺影響，
10 月年增率未發布。 
⚫ 10 月美國 PPI 月增 0.3%，較上月 1.4%明顯放緩，但年增率由 6.3%上
升至 6.5%，創 2022 年 11 月以來新高。能源與服務價格漲勢降溫，惟
食品、非能源商品及貨運價格續揚，顯示生產端通膨壓力仍高，後續須
關注成本轉嫁及對 Fed 利率政策之影響。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 2026 年 APEC 峰會將於 11 月 18 日至 19 日在中國深圳舉行。會前中美
相繼延長部分關稅豁免及貿易限制暫停措施至 2027 年 1 月，中國暫停
24%對美加徵關稅，惟保留 10%稅率，美國則延長 178 項中國商品關稅
豁免，釋出經貿緩和訊號，有助降低供應鏈不確定性。惟雙方結構性分
歧仍存，後續須關注川習會談成果及貿易政策走向。 
⚫ 輝達創辦人黃仁勳因婚外情及涉國安爭議引發調查，於 11 月 12 日辭去
執行長及董事職務，結束 33 年掌舵生涯，由財務長克雷斯暫代執行長。
消息重挫輝達股價及市值，並引發市場對全球 AI 產業、供應鏈及公司
治理風險的高度關注。 
短評 
⚫ 美國 10 月財政赤字顯著擴大，反映歲入下滑及政府支出負擔加重；成
屋銷售創多年低點，顯示高利率持續壓抑房市需求。儘管 CPI 及 PPI 月
增幅放緩，惟核心通膨及生產端價格壓力仍頑強，顯示物價上行風險尚

核心通膨(不含食品及能源)分別回落 0.2 個百分點為 0.7%、0.8%。

⚫ 由於國際能源價格上漲，進口增速高於出口，並抑制貿易順差擴張、減
緩外匯存底流入速度，惟此亦有助於減緩人民幣升值速度。 
⚫ 在民間消費乏力之下，企業仍大多自行吸收成本，加上政府控制零售燃
油及民生水電價格漲幅，故目前原油價格高漲僅少數反映於消費端。

一、 美國—10 月財政赤字大幅擴張、房市買氣探底，通膨壓力仍具韌性 
2026 年 10 月(2027 財年第 1 個月)聯邦政府財政赤字達 4,040.6 億美元，較去
年同期的 2,843.3 億美元大幅擴大 42.1% 。當月政府總收入為 3,689.4 億美元，年
減 8.8%，主因個人所得稅淨收入由去年同期的 2,169.5 億美元下滑至 1,863.8 億
美元，且 10 月關稅退稅高達 67.3 億美元(遠高於去年同期的 17.4 億美元)，大幅
抵銷了 259.5 億美元的關稅總收入，致使關稅淨收入雖較 9 月的 59.1 億美元回升
至 192.2 億美元，仍較去年同期的 313.5 億美元大減 38.7%。支出方面，10 月政
府總支出達 7,730.0 億美元，年增 12.2%，除因 11 月 1 日適逢非營業日，導致部
分現役與退役軍人薪資、退伍軍人福利及醫療保險等款項提前至 10 月撥付外，
醫療保險(1,700.5 億美元)、社會安全(1,426.9 億美元)及國防(1,288.8 億美元)等主
要項目均較去年同期成長；同時，10 月聯邦淨利息支出攀升至 1,030.8 億美元，
年增 13.4%。整體而言，在歲入下滑與福利、國防及利息支出擴張的雙重影響下，
美國新財年開局赤字顯著走高，截至 10 月底聯邦公債總額已進一步推升至 40.52
兆美元(其中公眾持有債務為 32.74 兆美元)，龐大債務規模與後續利息負擔仍將
是牽動美國財政穩健度的核心變數。 
2026 年 10 月美國成屋銷售經季節調整後年率降至 375 萬戶，較上月減少
5.5%，較去年同期下滑 8.8%，創下自 2010 年 8 月以來的最低水準，主因是 30 年
期固定房貸平均利率攀升至 7.33%(高於上月的 6.86%及去年同期的 6.25%)，使購
屋負擔加重，全美住房可負擔指數亦由去年同期的 107.3 降至 100.7。觀察區域表
現，10 月西部、南部及中西部銷量分別月減 9.7%、6.5%及 3.2%，僅東北部維持
持平，且四大地區銷量均較去年同期衰退。儘管買氣受抑，全美成屋售價中位數
仍年增 0.4%至 41.67 萬美元，寫下連續 40 個月年增紀錄；供給端則持續擴增，
待售庫存升至 159 萬戶，月增 1.3%、年增 4.6%，相當於 5.1 個月供給量(高於上
月的 4.7 個月與去年同期的 4.4 個月)。全美房地產經紀人協會(NAR)首席經濟學
家 Lawrence Yun 指出，在高利率衝擊下，10 月成屋市場呈現「量縮、價微揚、
庫存增」的格局，但全國 8,800 萬名屋主整體財務狀況依然穩健，不良資產交易
僅占 2%，考量目前就業市場仍持續成長，未來一旦房貸利率回落，市場可望釋

出可觀的積壓買氣。 
2026 年 10 月美國消費者物價指數(CPI)經季節調整後月增 0.4%，低於上月之
0.6%，惟高於市場預期的 0.3%；扣除食品與能源價格之核心 CPI 月增 0.3%，與
前月持平，並高於市場預期的 0.2%。在細項方面，住房指數月增 0.3%，貢獻整
體 CPI 月增幅逾三成，仍為推升通膨的重要因素；食品指數月增 0.5%，其中家庭
食品及外食價格分別上漲 0.4%及 0.5%；能源指數月增 0.7%，其中汽油價格及電
價分別上漲 1.0%及 0.9%，惟汽油漲幅較上月明顯放緩。核心項目方面，休閒娛
樂、醫療照護、汽車保險、家庭用品及教育等價格持續上揚，二手車及卡車價格
則下跌 1.2%，通訊價格亦小幅下降。另受 2025 年 10 月美國政府停擺影響，當月
未進行 CPI 調查並缺乏比較基期資料，美國勞工局未發布 2026 年 10 月整體 CPI
及多數主要指數之年增率。整體而言，10 月美國物價仍受住房、食品及能源價格
上漲帶動，核心 CPI 月增率則連續第三個月維持 0.3%，顯示服務類通膨壓力仍具
韌性，後續通膨走勢仍須持續觀察能源價格及住房成本變化。 
2026 年 10 月美國生產者物價指數(PPI)經季節調整後月增 0.3%，明顯低於上
月修正後之 1.4%，惟年增率由 6.3%升至 6.5%，創 2022 年 11 月以來最大漲幅，
顯示生產端價格壓力仍高。其中，最終需求商品價格月增 0.5%，低於上月之 2.5%，
逾六成漲幅來自不含食品及能源之商品價格上升，其中基本有機化學品價格上漲
3.9%；食品價格亦月增 1.3%，惟能源價格微降 0.1%，其中柴油價格下跌 7.9%。
另一方面，最終需求服務價格月增 0.2%，低於上月之 1.0%，主要受運輸及倉儲
服務價格上漲 2.0%帶動，其中貨運卡車運輸價格上漲 3.9%，貢獻服務價格漲幅
逾八成，惟貿易服務價格下降 0.2%，抵銷部分漲幅。剔除食品、能源及貿易服務
後之核心最終需求 PPI 月增 0.5%，與上月持平，年增率則由 5.0%小幅降至 4.9%。
整體而言，10 月美國 PPI 月增幅明顯放緩，主要反映能源價格回落及服務價格漲
勢降溫，惟食品、部分非能源商品及貨運價格仍持續上漲，加上整體 PPI 年增率
進一步攀升，顯示生產端通膨壓力仍高，後續須關注企業成本轉嫁情形、能源及
運輸價格變化，以及對消費者物價與聯準會利率政策之影響。 
2026 年亞太經濟合作組織(APEC)領袖峰會將於 11 月 18 日至 19 日在中國深
圳舉行。在峰會召開前及「川習三會」前夕，中美雙方相繼延長部分關稅豁免與
暫停措施，釋出緩和貿易緊張關係的訊號，並為後續經貿協商創造較有利條件。

消息隨即震撼全球科技與金融市場，導致輝達股價在 11 月 12 日開盤後盤中一度
重挫逾 14%、單日市值一度蒸發約 6,000 億美元，不僅重創高「含輝量」台股 ETF
表現，更引發外界對全球 AI 產業版圖及美中科技供應鏈穩定性的高度憂慮。儘
管黃仁勳矽谷盟友與部分員工近期發起連署與遊說，試圖推動他重返輝達，但美
國財政部長貝森特(Scott Bessent)已公開呼籲輝達領導層應進行「大幅改組」，使
後續接班與公司治理動向備受矚目。 
二、歐元區—勞動市場開始出現疲態 
 歐元區第三季 GDP 季增率二次估計值維持 0.4%不變，顯示在地緣政治不確
定性升高、能源價格再度上揚的環境下，整體景氣仍具支撐力；今年前三季經濟
成長率(年化率)則大約為 1.1%，大致持平去年水準。從需求面來看，政府支出與
民間消費為主要成長動能，其中民間消費受惠於較高的儲蓄水準及穩定的就業市
場，減輕能源價格上漲的衝擊，並且電動車、油電混合車需求持續增加，抵銷利
率升高及地緣政治波動對投資帶來的負面影響。

抑制薪資表現，並削弱消費信心，進而影響經濟復甦。 
三、日本—經濟成長停滯，內外需動能轉弱 
 2026 年第三季經濟成長季增率持平，結束連續三季的經濟擴張，主要受通膨、
部分地區自然災害及全球需求疲弱影響，導致私人消費季減 0.3%，出口亦下滑
0.2%，加上進口反彈 2.5%，使淨貿易拖累 GDP 達 0.6 個百分點；惟企業投資季
增 1.6%，為近三季以來首次成長，對景氣形成一定支撐。整體而言，日本內外需
動能均有所轉弱，後續民間消費回溫及企業投資能否持續，將是觀察經濟能否恢
復成長的重要指標。 
四、中國—國際能源價格上漲抑制貿易順差擴張 
10 月出口年增率較 9 月續增 1.6 個百分點至 37.9%，出口總值為 4,123 億美
元；累計前十個月出口年增率上升為 22.7%，累積出口總值為 37,827 億美元。前
十個月出口主要成長項目(累積年增率)包含 IC(111.1%)、自動數據處理設備
(60.6%)、船舶(27.7%)、汽車(52.2%)，其中 IC 及自動數據處理設備出口加速成長，
為 10 月出口年增率加速上升的主要因素。 
10 月進口年增率較 9 月增 10.6 個百分點至 41.6%，進口總值為 3,071 億美
元；累計前十個月進口值成長 29%至 27,401 億美元。該月原油、成品油進口金額
大幅增加，為帶動進口加速成長的主要因素；IC、自動數據處理設備(包含伺服器
等)進口分別成長 65.1%、136.7%，呼應中國對高階 IC 的依賴，以及部分伺服器
關鍵組件有賴於進口滿足所需。進出口相抵後，前十個月貿易順差 10,426 億美
元，僅較去年同期增加 8.7%，增幅明顯低於出口，顯示出口擴張同時伴隨關鍵原
物料進口大幅增加，稀釋出口成長的加值效果，亦反映部分高附加價值產品依賴
國際供應鏈支持。此外，因貿易順差增幅有限，10 月外匯存底僅增 49 億美元至
約 3.41 兆美元。 
10 月生產者通膨(出廠價格)由 9 月的 5%升至 5.7%，主因原油價格居高不下，
燃料購進成本持續上漲，提高生產成本所致。當月石油和天然氣開採業出廠價格

旅遊價格漲幅皆有放緩，其他商品及服務價格則大致平穩，使消費者通膨較前月
下滑。整體而言，雖然企業生產成本持續上升，但在民間消費乏力之下，企業仍
大多自行吸收成本，加上政府控制零售燃油及民生水電價格漲幅，故目前原油價
格高漲僅少數反映於消費端。惟考量能源成本傳導存在時間差，若油價維持高檔
或進一步上漲，未來通膨仍有進一步上升的風險。 
五、印度及東南亞—印度通膨超標；印尼、菲律賓第三季 GDP 分別達 5.5%及
2.1% 
(1)印度：10 月消費者物價指數（CPI）年增率進一步上揚至 6.09%，遠高於 9 月
的 5.54%，其中食品通膨加速上升至 8.5%，反映聖嬰現象導致季風降雨量低於預
期，影響農作物收成，同時核心通膨率（剔除汽油、柴油和貴金屬）從 9 月的 3.2%
上升至 3.8%，顯示潛在通膨持續走強並有所擴大。由於通膨率高於預期且突破央
行 6%的目標區間上限，預期印度央行 12 月的政策會議將有進一步升息的壓力。 
(2)印尼：2026 年第三季經濟成長率(GDP)年增 5.47%，私人消費成長 5.04%，主
要因政府採取措施，協助家庭抵禦通膨壓力和更高的借貸成本，惟受能源補貼負
擔加重導致財政空間收窄，政府支出大幅放緩至 8.95%，遠低於上季的 15.97%；
固定資產投資成長略放緩至 6.06%；另受石油和能源成本上漲影響，進口成長率
（7.31%）高於出口成長率（4.59%），致淨出口產生負貢獻，惟較上季對經濟的
拖累有所減緩。按產業別觀之，製造業(5.87%)、住宿業（11.22%）、運輸/倉儲業
（8.03%）和通訊業（7.13%）均有所成長，採礦業產出由負轉正 0.9%，抵銷農業
產出受聖嬰現象影響而大幅下降至 0.5%的影響。整體而言，第三季經濟成長優於
預期，惟在財政和貨幣政策收緊的背景下，國內需求可能進一步放緩。 
(3)菲律賓：2026 年第三季經濟成長率(GDP)年增 2.1%，為 2009 年第四季以來（不
計新冠疫情影響）最疲軟的經濟成長，低於政府先前已下調的全年 3.5%至 4.5%

### Page 10

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

9

的目標，聖嬰現象令農業產量低迷和能源成本高漲持續抑制經濟活動。家庭消費
成長放緩至 2.4%，政府支出成長降至 6.8%，固定投資雖小幅復甦仍萎縮 4.4%，
所幸出口成長 8.6%、進口成長放緩至 7.7%，致淨出口呈正向貢獻。按產業別觀
之，工業成長加快至 5.4%，服務業小幅放緩至 9%，惟農業產出受氣候影響而衰
退 0.4%。整體而言，第三季經濟活動主要受氣候干擾，農業、服務產出下滑並影
響消費支出，固定投資在公共建設投資帶動下有好轉跡象，然而央行升息提高借
貸成本，對私人投資產構成壓力，隨極端天氣事件的影響逐漸消退、家庭在年底
假期前消費意願提升、以及政府加快基礎設施建設，將有助於第四季經濟加快復
甦。 
六、台灣—AI 商機持續發酵，出口動能強勁 
10 月出口達 967.6 億美元，年增 58.9%，創歷史新高，主要由 AI 及高效能運
算需求帶動，其中資通與視聽產品、電子零組件合計約占總出口 83%，顯示 AI 伺
服器、半導體及相關零組件已成為出口成長核心。同時，對美出口達 329.3 億美
元、年增 55.9%，東協亦大幅成長 83.8%，反映 AI 需求及全球供應鏈重組正帶動
出口市場結構改變。惟進口年增 75.1%，增幅高於出口，後續宜觀察進口增加是
否有效轉化為產能及出口，以及 AI 需求、高基期與全球科技業資本支出變化對
出口動能的影響。

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Following on our results Memo for Andes, we widen our 
2026E net loss and maintain our Hold (3) rating. Management aims for a 
profitable 4Q26 to cover YTD losses, yet we are unsure how sustainable its 
bottom-line turnaround will be in the near term, given still uncertain timing 
of royalty conversion despite enriched IP and customer portfolios. That 
said, we remain structurally positive on Andes’s outlook, thanks to its 
leading position in the RISC-V architecture well prepared to capitalise on 
the AI cycle from cloud to edge to physical.

What’s the impact: Forecast changes in a mixed bag. We widen our 
2026E net loss for Andes to factor in its higher-than-expected opex YTD, 
but we keep our 2027E EPS largely unchanged to factor in its frugal opex 
control starting from 3Q26. Though bottom-line turnaround looks still 
bumpy in the near term on its suboptimal operating leverage due to still 
suboptimal scale, as a result of still uncertain timing of royalty revenue 
conversion, we merit management’s opex control to speed up bottom-line 
turnaround. This, together with its enriched RISC-V IP portfolio and 
broadened customer base for market-share gain, should help sustain its 
turnaround from 2H27 when topline expands to improve earnings quality 
and investor confidence.

Patience still for a sustainable turnaround. Despite this year’s business 
hiccups on delayed royalty ramp and opex volatility, we remain positive on 
Andes’s structural outlook, given its leading position in the RISC-V 
architecture offering enriched high-performance/low-power compute IP as 
substitutes to Arm and x86 CPU counterparts to capitalise on the secular AI 
cycle, with a broad coverage of applications from accelerated compute and 
embedded control to automotive. Management’s comments support our 
view, as it expects the RISC-V’s open-source ecosystem to drive innovation 
allowing designs of instruction sets tailored to customers’ specific needs, 
which should help enhance customer stickiness while attracting new 
customers for market-share gains. That said, patience may be still needed 
for Andes’s monetisation to bear fruit for a sustainable turnaround.

What we recommend: Despite our forecast revisions, we raise our 12M 
TP to TWD240 (from TWD180), still based on DCF methodology (WACC: 
18%, terminal growth: 3%) which we believe is appropriate for a startup IP 
company such as Andes. Given the still bumpy road to any sustainable 
turnaround, we maintain our Hold (3) call with key upside/downside risks of 
any faster RISC-V ecosystem build/further delays of royalty ramp.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E EPS are 29-48% above the consensus 
forecast likely due to our lower opex assumptions for Andes.

17 November 2026

Near-term turnaround still looks bumpy

➢ 2026E net loss likely to widen on opex volatility but … 
➢ … no harm to structural turnaround from 2H27 
➢ Maintaining our Hold (3) rating with a higher 12M TP of TWD240

Company profile 
Founded in 2005, Andes is an embedded microprocessor intellectual property (IP) provider with 
solutions in high-performance and low-power 32/64-bit processors and associated SoC platforms. Its 
solutions can be adopted for diverse applications such as IoT, 5G, AI, Bluetooth/WiFi devices, gaming 
and automotive. As a founding member of RISC-V International, Andes focuses on developing 
processor IPs based on the RISC-V instruction-set architecture while its mature V3 architecture is the 
main contributor of its royalty revenue.

### Page 5

5

Andes Technology (6533 TT): 17 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 17 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
Andes’ Board of Directors consists of seven members, including three independent directors,
representing approximately 43% of the total board composition, exceeding the regulatory
minimum requirement of 20%. The roles of Chairman and President are held by separate
individuals, reflecting a clear division of leadership responsibilities.
Currently, the Board includes one female director among the seven seats. Andes acknowledges
that gender diversity at the board level remains an area for improvement and is actively
committed to strengthening diversity and inclusion as part of its ongoing governance
enhancement efforts.
Capital management 1
As a semiconductor IP provider without physical manufacturing operations, Andes does not
require significant capital expenditure for factories. Instead, its primary capital needs are related
to ongoing investment in research and development to support new IP development and capture
emerging market opportunities. From this perspective, we believe Andes is inclined to retain a
portion of its earnings to fund future IP innovation rather than fully distributing profits to
shareholders.
Andes did not distribute dividends prior to 2019. In 2019, it paid out 72% of its 2018 net profit as
dividends. Subsequently, the dividend payout ratio remained high at approximately 90% during
the period from 2020 to 2022. However, the payout ratio declined sharply to 21% in 2023 based
on 2022 net profit, reflecting a more conservative capital allocation approach.
Due to weakened profitability, Andes did not distribute dividends in 2024 or 2025, as earnings
were insufficient to support shareholder payouts. Management has prioritized financial flexibility
and the preservation of resources to navigate challenging market conditions and sustain long-
term R&D investment.
Related party &
transaction 1
Before July 2021, Andes conducted related party transactions with MediaTek (2454 TT) given
each company's chairman was the same person. MediaTek was Andes' largest customer with a
31% revenue contribution in 2013. Its revenue contribution has gradually declined in past years
to a single digit percentage in 2021. Since Andes' board of directors' election in July 2021, its
chairman and MediaTek's chairman are no longer the same person. Andes currently does not
have any meaningful related party transactions.
S Supply chain management 1
As a semiconductor IP provider without physical products, Andes mainly procures softwares &
services from its suppliers. Andes implements supplier management policies to ensure that there
is no violation of corporate social responsibility policy in the procurement contract with its
suppliers.
E Water & wastewater management 1
Andes is a semiconductor IP provider without any physical products. Thus, it does not have on-
site manufacturing activities to weigh on the environment. It has set up a goal to reduce water
consumption by 2% every year through measures such as installing faucet water savers and
promoting the practice of saving water.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1 Andes is a semiconductor IP provider without any physical products. Thus, it does not have on- site manufacturing activities to weigh on the environment.
E GHG emissions 1
Andes is a semiconductor IP provider without any physical products. Thus, it does not have on-
site manufacturing activities to weigh on the environment. However, the company still applies
measures to reduce power consumption for its operations, such as turning off lights one hour per
day during the lunch break. It has set up a goal to reduce its total electricity consumption by 1-
2% every year.

Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
Limited direct impact on Aspeed and Montage in the near term,

but we see potential long-term demand risks.
The US BIS has tightened export controls to restrict Chinese companies' remote
access to advanced computing capacity through third-country data centers, while
adding licensing requirements for advanced chips shipped to key Southeast Asian
markets, including Singapore and Malaysia (link).
Our view: Gary Yu (China Internet Analyst; link) sees this as another step toward
closing loopholes in Al compute access, potentially slowing GPU deployment by

China-linked cloud operators and raising compliance costs for overseas Al
infrastructure investment. New supplies of advanced chipsets within China to
become increasingly limited. For cloud semis, we see limited direct impact on
Aspeed and Montage in the near term but there are potential long-term demand
risks. Aspeed has a high share in GPU and China server platforms. Montage
interface and retimer chips may also be impacted over the long term.
Please also refer to our recent notes: Cloud Semis: Peak Growth, Less Upside;

Investor pushback on our cloud semis/old memory downgrades.

export control restrictions. Readers are solely responsible for ensuring that their
investment or trade activities are carried out in compliance with applicable laws.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

Feedback:  Read across from Nan
Ya New Material's comments for
our coverage
Substrate CCL: Nan Ya New Material (not covered) has started shipping BT
substrate CCL in small volumes, primarily to domestic customers. We believe this is

driven by raw material supply constraints and has positive implications to non-
Japanese substrate CCL suppliers, including Elite Material, as customers diversify
away from incumbent suppliers.
Glass cloth: Nan Ya New Material expects the price trend of 7628-type glass cloth
to normalize from 2Q27 onwards, while thin/super-thin E glass cloth  remains very
under-supplied in CY27, which should provide support to thin E glass cloth pricing
through next year. The implication to Kingboard is therefore mixed.

Copper foil: Nan Ya New Material sees HTE copper foil declining while its demand
for advanced RTF and HVLP copper foils continues to surge, which bodes well for
Co-Tech, which has exited HTE copper foil business to focus on advanced RTF and
HVLP. The company also sources high-end HVLP from Co-Tech.
Customers: Nan Ya New Material mentioned major Chinese PCB vendors are all its
customers, including Shennan, WUS, Shengyi Elec, Founder, Kinwong, etc. For the

overseas market, it is also engaged with ISU, TTM and Unimicron, primarily for North
America-based customers.
Order change? Nan Ya New Material has not seen any server-related order cuts so
far; it noted the PCB/CCL  demand from the China compute market is growing
strongly. This also has positive implications to Chinese PCB/CCL suppliers Shennan
and Shengyi.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
                        Shennan Circuits Co Ltd (002916.SZ)                    
Base case, derived from a residual income (RI) model. Key assumptions include a cost of equity 
of 6.7% (beta of 1.1, equity premium of 5%, and risk-free rate of 1.3%), 7% medium-term growth 
rate, and 4% terminal growth rate.
                        Risks to Upside                    
n Rising demand for 5G and data center PCB.
n Further share gain for 5G and data center PCB.
n Rising IC substrate demand driven by acceleration in China's semiconductor localiza-
tion.
                        Risks to Downside                    
n Rising competition for 5G and data center PCB.
n Delay in 5G network deployment.
n Escalating US-China trade tensions.

of 9.7% (beta of 1.0, equity premium of 8.7% and risk-free rate of 1%), medium-term growth 
rate of 8%, and terminal growth rate of 3%.
                        Risks to Upside                    
n Stronger AI demand

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

研 究 報 告                
旺矽(6223.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
探針卡需求強勁挹注短期營運動能強勁、CPO 設備進度如期  
 投資評價與建議 
目前探針卡廠商評價普遍修正至20~35倍，尤其以短期營運相對穩健之廠商
(如精測、旺矽)仍相對有撐，而我們觀察公司探針卡需求強勁支撐短期營運動
能強勁，並重申CPO設備驗證進度如期、客戶數持續增加，故仍看好評價仍有
望回到合理區間之中上緣。 
 重點評論分析 
4Q26營收有望雙位數季增：公司預估4Q26營收有望季增5~10%、毛利率
維持近3Q26之高檔水準，觀察探針卡BB Ratio均維持高檔水準，且擴產
計畫不變。基於公司10月營收達22.3億元創下新高、財測⼀向較為保守，
預估營收有望季增12.7%至66.6億元，毛利率61.8%，稅後EPS 21.82元。 
積極擴增MEMS產能、重申CPO客戶需求與進度如期：展望2027年：
(1)VPC和MEMS明年底有望擴至250萬針、600萬針，擴充幅度仍具有上
修空間，當前VPC主要客戶未有升級至MEMS規劃，至於MEMS主要貢獻
則來自於美系客戶伺服器CPU；(2)insertion 2客戶將於12月通知驗證結
果，1H27有望獲得量產機訂單，至於insertion 3已如期出貨工程機給3間
客戶，並預期1H27將陸續獲得量產機訂單，惟公司預估CPO設備營收顯
著貢獻時間點將落在2028年，故我們同步調整營收、獲利。 
 投資風險 
CPO進展不如預期、探針卡擴產針數下修。

### Page 2

2

| 營運概況 
3Q26獲利優於預期：3Q26營收季增12.8%至59億元，連續七季創下歷史新高，毛利率61.7%，稅後EPS 19.18元，
其中營收、獲利均優於預期，推估主因為探針卡產品組合改善，加上於2Q26進行之探針卡報價調整完整貢獻3Q
營收(包含CPC漲價20%、取消VPC和MEMS 10%價格優惠)，另公司營業費用管控得宜也同步挹注獲利。 
估4Q26營收有望雙位數季增：公司預估4Q26營收有望季增5~10%、毛利率有望維持近3Q26之高檔水準，我們
推估動能包含：(1)維持VPC、MEMS月產能今年底分別擴至200萬針、350萬針之進展；(2)訂單出貨則觀察探針卡
BB Ratio均維持高檔水準，其中CPC今年前10個月維持滿載、目前BB Ratio接近1，至於MEMS、VPC則超過1，
考量公司探針卡交期已明顯延⻑至4~6個月，隱含本季探針卡營收仍有望持續提升。基於公司10月營收達22.3億
元創下新高、財測⼀向較為保守，預估本季營收有望季增12.7%至66.6億元，毛利率61.8%，稅後EPS 21.82元。 
積極擴增MEMS產能、重申CPO客戶需求與進度如期：展望2027年，我們觀察：(1)探針卡中，明年CPC月產能維
持50萬針、VPC和MEMS明年底則有望擴至250萬針、600萬針，產能擴充幅度具有上修空間，且強調2028年維持
積極擴產策略，訂單貢獻中，VPC主要客戶近年未有升級至MEMS探針卡之規劃，預期該客戶AI ASIC訂單將支撐
明年VPC需求，至於MEMS明年主要貢獻則來自於美系客戶伺服器CPU，且公司持續爭取潛在客戶AI ASIC訂單，
另規劃於2H27量產之PCB廠有望挹注探針卡成本優化；(2)CPO設備中，insertion 2如我們先前所提，於3Q26底
～4Q26初送樣至客戶端進行驗證，公司預估客戶將於12月通知驗證結果，根據目前客戶回饋正向，預期1H27有
望獲得量產機訂單，此外近期有另⼀客戶與公司接洽中(公司預估1H27獲得驗證結果)，至於insertion 3則已如期
出貨工程機給3間客戶，也將陸續交機給另3間客戶，預計1H27將陸續獲得insertion 3量產機訂單，惟考量接單至
出貨、裝機驗收之時間較⻑，公司認為CPO相關設備顯著營收貢獻時間點將落在2028年，我們則同步下修明年相
關設備之營收認列數量。綜上所述，公司預估明年營收有望年增至少50%、毛利率維持在60%以上；我們則預估
明年營收有望年增58.6%至344.6億元，較先前下修主要反映部分CPO設備遞延至2028年認列營收，毛利率63.2%，
稅後EPS 117.77元。

6415 矽力杰( Silergy Corp.)

報告日期：2026/11/16          研究員：王蕎程(Joe Wang) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要: 
 本業獲利持續改善：3Q26 營業利益季增 29.7%，營益率提升
至 13.26%；惟 2Q26 受金融資產評價利益挹注，墊高比較基
期，第三季獲利下滑主要來自業外收益回落，並非本業衰退。 
 AIDC 與車用雙引擎帶動成長：AIDC 營收年增逾 120%，新一
代 SPS 預計 2027 年開始放量；車用受惠 MCU 及高壓大電流
產品導入，帶動單車價值提升，預期 2027 年兩大業務營收均
有望成長逾 50%。 
 製程升級與供貨優勢支撐成長：Gen4 產品持續放量，預期 2027
年底營收占比達 50%，搭 配 12 吋產能布局及 Gen5 新品量產，
有利爭取大型客戶，惟封測產能吃緊及成本上升仍為毛利改善
的觀察重點。

一、3Q26 本業獲利持續改善，預估 4Q26 EPS 2.5 元 
矽力*-KY 為電源管理 IC 設計廠，3Q26 營收 72.59 億元（QoQ 
+20.9%、YoY +52.4%），主要受惠 AIDC、工業及車用需求成長，
以及消費電子客戶提前拉貨。毛利率 47.40%，QoQ +0.4 個百分點，
主要因產品組合改善；營業利益 9.63 億元（QoQ +29.7%），營業
利益率 13.26%，QoQ +0.88 個百分點，本業獲利持續改善。惟 2Q26
受金融資產評價利益 9.59 億元挹注，墊高獲利基期，3Q26 因業外
損益回落至 1.11 億元，稅後淨利降至 9.09 億元（QoQ -42.1%），
EPS 2.34 元，獲利下滑主要來自業外收益減少，並非本業衰退。
展望 4Q26，消費電子需求受第三季提前拉貨及終端市場疲弱影
響，手機、電視等產品出貨轉弱，惟資料中心需求仍維持成長，
預估營收 74.63 億元，毛利率 47.80%，稅後 EPS 2.50 元。

二、產品組合優化，AIDC 與車用為明年主要成長動能 
AIDC 為公司主要成長來源，3Q26 營收占比達 17%，YoY +120%。
隨 AI 伺服器 CPU 及 GPU 功耗提高，主板供電系統需要處理更大
電流，同時降低電力損耗及散熱負擔，帶動高效率電源管理 IC 需
求。其中，新一代 SPS（Smart Power Stage）將功率元件、驅動器
及感測功能整合於單一封裝，可改善高電流供電效率並縮小電路
板空間，目前已進入多家客戶驗證階段，預期 2027 年開始貢獻營
收。公司表示，北美客戶對相關產品的需求較為迫切，預期放量
速度將快於中國客戶；此外，伺服器主板、資料中心通訊設備及
SSD 為 AIDC 主要成長應用，三者合計可占 AI 相關營收約 70%～

三、車用單車價值提升，光通訊與 12 吋製程布局 
車用方面，公司持續增加單一車款的晶片供應種類，除既有電源
管理產品外，高壓、大電流電源 IC、MCU 及電池管理相關產品陸
續導入域控制器、電驅等應用，帶動單車產品價值提升。公司表
示，高階車款單車產品價值預計由 2026 年底的 200～220 美元，提
升至 2027 年的 270～300 美元；海外新客戶近期已開始小批量出
貨，明年貢獻可望增加，惟中國客戶仍為主要營收來源，預期 2027
年車用營收成長逾 50%。光通訊方面，隨 AI 資料中心傳輸速率提
升，光模組對供電效率、功耗及散熱的要求同步提高，帶動高效
率電源轉換、雷射驅動電源及整合式電源管理產品需求。公司已
布局 NPO 及 CPO 相關產品，並進入客戶設計導入階段。

製程方面，公司布局 12 吋晶圓產能，目前的產能可支應至少 20
億美元營收規模。隨 AIDC 及車用需求增加，類比 IC 供應鏈產能
趨緊，部分同業仍以 8 吋製程為主，矽力則因 12 吋產能準備較為
充足，有利爭取重視供貨穩定性的大型客戶。公司表示，Gen4 產
品持續放量，預期 2027 年底營收占比約 50%，Gen5 則預計於 2027
年量產。新製程除改善成本結構外，也能支援 AIDC 對電源轉換效
率、功率密度及產品整合度的要求，有利提升高階產品比重。

供應鏈方面，目前主要瓶頸在封裝測試，受設備供應不足及裝機
速度限制，產品生產週期由原先約 4 個月延長至 9 個月，公司須
提前備妥晶圓等待後段排產，帶動在製品增加。預期 2027 年上半
年封測產能仍難明顯改善，較大幅度的產能紓解可能落在 2027 年
下半年至 2028 年。雖然公司前段晶圓供應相對充足，但後段產能
限制仍可能影響出貨速度，且成本上升未必能完全轉嫁客戶。

四、預估 2026 / 2027 年稅後 EPS 10.87 / 14.72 元 
預估 2026 年營收 255.85 億元（YoY +36.0%），毛利率 47.58%，營
業利益 31.33 億元，稅後 EPS 10.87 元。展望 2027 年，受惠 AIDC
及車用新品放量、Gen4 良率改善與高階產品比重提升，預估營收
319.81 億元（YoY +25.0%），毛利率提升至 50.07%；營業利益 57.83
億元（YoY +84.6%）。考量業外收益回歸正常水準，預估稅後 EPS 
14.72 元（YoY +35.4%）。 
五、投資建議「買進」，目標價 600 元 
綜合考量：(1) AIDC 需求持續成長，帶動伺服器主板、SSD 及光

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一. 公司簡介 
矽力杰股份有限公司（簡稱：矽力 KY；代號：6415）主要從事類
比、數位混和 IC 之研發設計及銷售，產品應用範圍廣泛，主要
應用於 3C 產業、雲端伺服器、工業類、汽車、5G 通訊等相關
領域，包括 LED 照明、平板電腦、筆記型電腦、固態硬碟、視
頻監控、伺服器、數位機上盒、智能 音箱、智慧型手機、多媒體
電視、LED 背光模組、路由器、行動電源、汽車電子、通訊基地
台及智能電錶等，並持續增加各種終端應用，如 TV Panel、手機
快充、醫療領域等。

（資料來源：矽力 KY 2025 年報）

矽力 KY 目前產品主要是類比 IC 晶片，產品廣泛應用於消費性
電子產品、工業用產品、資訊產品、5G 網路通訊產品及汽車電子
產品。針對終端產品設計需求，該公司可提供不同的晶片，例如
平板電腦、電池充電管理晶片、直流對直流轉換晶片、過流保護
晶片、靜電防護裝置、LED 背光驅動晶片以及 PMU 等；針對 LED 
照明，該公司可提供 LED 照明驅動晶片；針對機上盒，該公司
可提供交流對直流轉換晶片，直流對直流轉換晶片，過流保護晶
片，EMI 防護裝置等；智慧電表，則提供包含能源監控管理(Energy 
measurement IC)的系統晶片 System on Chip (SOC) 以及高精度電
流檢測方案。

二. 產業趨勢概況 
市場概況 
(1)半導體市場概況與 IC 設計業 
半導體產業為工業類設備、資訊電腦、網路通訊、消費性及汽車
電子產品應用的關鍵性產業，亦是目前主導性的基礎產業之一，
隨著 3C(電腦 Computer、通訊 Communication、消費 Consumer)產
品的蓬勃發展和工業類產品智慧化和低能耗的需求，產品生命週
期縮短而更加快速，並朝多元化及豐富化發展，隨著產品推陳出
新與成長，均促進未來半導體需求及市場穩定發展。國內半導體
產業鏈發展日益成熟，有完整的上、中、下游分工體系，使得 IC 
設計公司得以專精於從事設計工作，再將 IC 的製造委託給晶圓
代工製作成晶圓半成品，經過前段測試後再交由封裝廠進行切割
及封裝，最後再經專業測試廠做後段測 試後，完成最後產品，也
造就了良好的 IC 設計產業發展環境。 IC 元件一般可分為數位
邏輯 IC 及類比 IC 兩大類。數位 IC 利用 0、1 非連續性方式
傳遞訊號，多用於處理數位資訊如計算、控制、儲存等，強調尺
寸、速度與功耗(Process Driven)。類比 IC 則負責處理來自自然界
的連續性類比訊號，如光、 熱、電、速度、壓力等物理量，並作
為橋接上述各類物理訊息與數位電子系統的媒介，需同時藉由製
程、電路設計和半導體元件物理的相互配合，在晶片效能及成本 
上尋求最適化，由於其決定了數位產品所能呈現的最終呈現品

### Page 5

Company Report 
公司研究報告 
本報告僅供宏遠投顧內部及客戶參考，雖已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場或個股之相關
條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保證。非經本公司同意，不得
將本報告加以引用或轉載。 
5 
質，因此更為注重元件的特性(Design Driven)，琢磨於可靠度、穩
定度、功率消耗、能源轉換效率、電壓電流控制能力等

(2)電源管理 IC 
隨著環保與節能議題發酵，節能科技為目前重要發展課題之一，
而類比 IC 中 的電源管理 IC 正扮演著不可或缺的關鍵性角
色，電源管理 IC 主要作為在不同的系統運作狀態下，隨時控制
並保持系統內適當電流與電壓正常供給。而電源供應器的好壞，
將會直接影響電子產品的使用壽命。近年來電源供應器均朝向輕
薄短小、省電、耐用的方向發展。而要產品達到這些目標的重要
關鍵，乃在於如何把電源供應器做到高效率(高效能)、高穩壓率和
低雜訊干擾，這其中所有參與管制功能的 IC 即被稱為「電源控
制、管理類比 IC」，每年市場均保持穩定成長，消費電子、5G 網
路通信、移動互聯領域、雲端伺服器、工業應用都是目前最主要
市場，未來並朝向人工智慧及電動車及車連網方向發展。電源管
理單元(PMU)是一種高度集成的電源管理方案，將傳統分立的若干
類電源管理器件整合在單個的封裝之內，可實現更高的電源轉換
效率、更低功耗，以及更少的元件數。PMU 在有限的體積內集成
了盡可能多的電源通道，根據各個子單元的需求、提供高效率的
供電，或提供低雜訊的乾淨電源，又或者是系統必須的上電時序
控制，成為為系統供電的首選。過去十年智慧型手持式裝置在市
場快速發展，總體來看，終端市場整體還將保持快速成長，在此
大背景下，PMU 的市場需求有望實現較之前市場預期更高的發展
態勢。隨著智慧型手持式產品的大螢幕化、輕薄化，電池的體積
也將會越來越小，在相應的電池容量不變的情況下，要求整機的
續航能力要更強，除了不斷降低晶片功耗改善以外，對電源的智
慧化管理也變得不可或缺，因此未來 PMU 市場還會有不錯發展。

未來展望 
電源管理 IC 是類比 IC 最基礎和重要的產品，近年來電源管理
一直是半導體領域的熱門市場之一，且電源管理 IC 是所有電子
產品必備的關鍵零組件，屬於高功率類比電路技術，主要用途為
提供電子產品穩定且適當電壓的電源，將直接影響電子產品的使
用壽命。隨著全球 3C 產品的功能不斷增加，汽車自動駕駛和工
業自動 化需求，終端應用漸漸走向低耗電、輕薄短小與多功能整
合以及對產品壽命與可靠度要求更高的趨勢，為達到此目的，電
源管理 IC 必須做到使電源系統效率提升、節能及縮小 IC 元件
封裝以減少所佔用之電路板面積等重點。另隨著 CPU 不斷向上 
躍進，對電源的穩定性及電壓的精準度也就更為要求。此外，高
整合度不但能減少零件數量，達到降低系統耗電以及提升系統可
靠度及品質等效益，並可提高生產良率，且降低生產工時，進而

### Page 6

Company Report 
公司研究報告

條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保證。非經本公司同意，不得
將本報告加以引用或轉載。 
6 
降低成本。而隨著環保與節能的議題發酵，節能科技為目前重要
發展課題之一，電源管理 IC 扮演不可或缺的關鍵角色。除低耗
電、極低的靜態消耗電流等需求外，低耗電使用無鉛封裝製程亦
是全球各國對環保、低耗能等綠色概念的要求重點，而電源管理 
IC 同時也得考量未來環保法規帶來的限制，故符合環保及綠色能
源將成為電源管理 IC 設計的重要方向。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

一、預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 2.50 元 
矽力*-KY 3Q26 營收 72.59 億元（QoQ +20.9%、YoY +52.4%），受
惠 AIDC、工業及車用需求成長，毛利率 47.40%，營業利益 9.63
億元（QoQ +29.7%）；惟 2Q26 金融資產評價利益墊高比較基期，
3Q26 業外收益回落，稅後淨利 9.09 億元（QoQ -42.1%），EPS 2.34
元。展望 4Q26，消費電子需求轉弱，惟資料中心需求維持成長，
預估營收 74.63 億元（QoQ +2.8%），毛利率 47.80%，稅後淨利 9.76
億元，EPS 2.50 元。

二、預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 10.87 元 
預估 2026 年營收 255.85 億元（YoY +36.0%），受惠 AIDC 及車用
需求增加，毛利率 47.58%，營業毛利 121.74 億元；營業利益 31.33
億元，營業利益率 12.25%，業外損益 15.70 億元，稅後淨利 42.24
億元，稅後 EPS 10.87 元。

三、預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 14.72 元 
展望 2027 年，受惠 AIDC 新一代 SPS 產品導入、車用單車價值提
升，以及 Gen4 製程良率改善與高階產品比重增加，預估全年營收
319.81 億元（YoY +25.0%），營業毛利 160.12 億元，毛利率 50.07%；
營業利益 57.83 億元（YoY +84.6%），營業利益率 18.08%，業外
損益 4.51 億元，稅後淨利 57.34 億元（YoY +35.7%），稅後 EPS 14.72
元。

Document 362: First Impressions_ NZ retail trade, September quarter 2026 - New Zealanders fill up the car and head back to the mall (2026-11-25)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ retail trade, September quarter 2026 - New Zealanders fill up the car and head back to the mall (2026-11-25).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期

### Page 1

#retail #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ retail trade, September quarter 2026 - New
Zealanders fill up the car and head back to the mall
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:19 November 23 2026 Share
The September quarter saw a larger than expected rise in spending, including firmness in
core categories.
September quarter retail sales
Retail sales (volume of goods sold): +2.2% (Prev: -0.4%)

### Page 2

Westpac f/c: 1.4%, Market: +0.5% 
Core retail sales (volume of goods sold): 1.4% (Prev: 0.8%)

Nominal retail sales: 2.4% (Prev: 1.0%)

Year to September
Volume of goods sold: 3.5%
Nominal sales: 7.0%

New Zealanders fill up the car and head back to the mall.
The September quarter retail sales report showed a sharp recovery in spending. Nominal spending
was up 2.4% over the quarter, and the amount of goods that New Zealanders are actually taking home
was up a solid 2.2%.
Today’s result was above our top of market forecast for a 1.4% quarterly rise, and well above the
median market forecast for +0.5%.
While the strong September quarter result is welcome news, we are taking it with a grain of salt. Much
of the rise in spending was related to fuel spending, which was boosted by the fall in fuel prices over
the September quarter, and fuel prices have step higher since then.
However, even excluding fuel, we have seen firmness in spending appetites. Core spending (excl. fuel
and motor vehicles) was up 1.4% over the September quarter and is 4.7% higher than this time last
year. 
Looking at the breakdown of spending, we did see a rise in grocery purchases. Meanwhile, spending in
discretionary categories was mixed – there has been lower spending on items like furnishings and
hardware, while spending on hospitality, electronics and recreational items is up. Overall, were left with
a picture of gradually improving spending appetites.

Outlook
Today’s report reinforces our expectations that the economy is moving into stronger phase. We expect
that spending levels will continue to firm through the holiday season and 2027. However, that recovery
is still likely to be gradual due to the combination of ongoing high inflation, a soft labour market and
rising interest rates. We also expect the recovery will be uneven, with those regions with strong links to
the agricultural and tourism sectors outperforming metro regions, like Auckland and Wellington.

Implications for GDP growth
We’re forecasting GDP growth of 0.6% in the September quarter. Today’s result was above
expectations. We’ll take a closer look at how our forecast for GDP growth is shaping up over the next
couple of weeks as additional data on September quarter activity is released.

Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist

will still head in the wrong direction. That needs to change

The 10yr yield held in the mid-to-low 4.9% area, with, if anything, a
tendency to test lower in yield, which is the reverse of what would
be expected from a resumed risk-on day. The movement in the
price of oil had a more signiﬁcant correlation eﬀect, with Brent
managing to dip below $90/bbl. This is clearly helpful for headline
inﬂation rates down the line, and will show up in the December
inﬂation readings (reﬂecting November).
That said, the October personal consumption expenditure (PCE)

inﬂation reading is due on Wednesday, and it's not going to be
pretty. The good news is the market should already be anticipating
an acceleration in core PCE inﬂation to 3.2% year-on-year (YoY) (up
from 3.1% YoY). The bad news is this is still a high number, and it
continues to head in the wrong direction. Also, the fall in energy
prices will have a far less direct eﬀect on core PCE. Ultimately, it will,
of course, have some eﬀect, as, for example, airline fares can ease.

That apart, the bond market remains in a troubling environment
over the coming months. We suspect that, while the risk-on mode
seen on Monday may not be repeated immediately, the recent sell-
oﬀs in the AI space are, in fact, healthy over the medium term. They
can ultimately result in risk coming back on by the turn of the year.
On top of that, ﬁscal narratives stretching from Germany (defence)
to France (already bloated) to the US (untamed) are all adding to a

sense of heightened risks for core bonds. The big swing factor could
be inﬂation. But it's just too early to declare victory on that front
yet. So, we maintain a cautious bond view for the next few months.

Markets will likely remain focused on the bounce back in AI
stocks. Eurozone data includes Spanish PPIs for October,
which, given the focus on second-round eﬀects, will be
watched. Other releases include Germany's Ifo survey results.

in November. From the US we have the Conference Board

Document 450: Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign Exchange Assets (2026-11-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign Exchange Assets (2026-11-12).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 17
Status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign
Exchange Assets
A trillion-dollar gap in foreign exchange purchases raises questions about where
China’s foreign currency assets are held.
Carlos Rawlins/Reuters
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
PUBLISHED
November 11, 2026 8:32 p.m.
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
The PBOC argues—unpersuasively, I think—that it
doesn’t have any hidden foreign exchange reserves. I
would reframe that as hidden foreign currency assets,
as foreign currency lent out (or swapped out) with
domestic financial institutions is a classic way of
masking reserves.
Many in Asia—and some in the IMF—are inclined
to believe the PBOC. Fair enough, it is hard to

challenge an institution as large and powerful (in
some ways) as the PBOC.

### Page 3

3
But to convince, the PBOC really needs to provide
answers to three questions.
1. What explains the current gap between foreign
exchange settlement and the PBOC’s reserves?
Settlement is the purchase of foreign exchange in the
market by the PBOC and the state banks. It
technically implies taking an open foreign currency
position, as the foreign exchange is being bought with
renminbi.
Historically, it correlated with changes in the PBOC’s
balance sheet.

And some gaps before 2012 can be explained. In late
2005 and 2006, the PBOC did over $100 billion in
swaps with the state banks, who used the FX to buy
foreign bonds (portfolio debt outflows surged). Those

### Page 4

4
swaps were unwound after the global financial crisis,
and actually provided some of the foreign currency
that “funded” the China Investment Corporation
when it initially scaled up.
And in 2007 and 2008, the PBOC required that the
state banks hold FX against their domestic RMB
deposits (to keep the FX out of reserves; it was held
on deposit at the PBOC and shows up as “other
foreign assets” on the PBOC’s balance sheet and as
“other, other, short-term” in the balance of payments.*

After 2018, though, there are unexplained gaps
between the FX bought in settlement and the
PBOC’s balance sheet (technically cumulative
monthly changes to capture the fact that FX is held as
its initial CNY purchase price).
There was a net accumulation of FX outside the
PBOC’s balance sheet in 2020 and 2021, when the
CNY was under pressure to appreciate. There are
modest sales in 2023 and 2024 when there is
depreciation pressure. And there are large purchases

in 2025 and the first 9 months of 2026—total
purchases in settlement in the last 12 months (spot)
are $500 to 600 billion, while the increase in the
PBOC’s balance sheet is only around $50 billion over
the same period.

### Page 5

5
The gap over time is now $1 trillion. The PBOC
needs to be able to answer a simple question: who
holds this FX, and who holds the associated FX
market risk (given the FX has been purchased with
RMB).
The monthly pattern of growth in settlement strongly
suggests it is backdoor intervention; there are sales
when spot is weak (economically) relative to the fix,
and purchases when spot is either at the fix or just
stronger than the fix (the strong edge of the band

hasn’t been used). The pattern of purchases and sales
thus looks exactly like that of a central bank
maintaining a tight band around a (crawling) peg.
If the PBOC cannot credibly explain this gap, there
isn’t much more to discuss. The PBOC argues that it
hasn’t swapped FX with the state banks or put foreign
currency on deposit at the state banks. Fair enough.

### Page 6

6
But someone is buying in settlement; the PBOC
ought to know who…
2. Missing interest income
There is an obvious tell here—Bloomberg reported
back in 2022 that the PBOC transferred $158 billion
in accumulated interest income to the Ministry of
Finance as part of a special dividend to hold the
budget deficit down. Again, fair enough. But
absolutely nothing changed in the FX reserves held
on the PBOC’s balance sheet or in the balance of
payments.
That highlights a broader issue. Since the end of

2016, the PBOC’s balance sheet FX hasn’t really
changed.

### Page 7

7
Peer institutions generally have an upward slope to
their reserves as a result of “gains from reserve
management” or “reinvestment of interest income.” **
The Central Bank of China (Taipei) and Japan’s
Ministry of Finance are good examples.
The interest on ~$3 trillion in reserves should be
around $100 billion a year. A bit less during the “low-
for-long” era before the pandemic, when SAFE wasn’t
getting much income out of its euro portfolio. A bit

more now, when U.S. short-term rates are well above 3
percent and SAFE’s legacy agencies carry yields of 5
or 6 percent. $100 billion over 10 years is real money
—roughly a trillion dollars.
If SAFE has steadily been selling its interest income,
it should say so (the fact that BOP reserves have often
diverged from the PBOC balance sheet suggests a
more complex story).

### Page 8

8
Moreover, if SAFE has been transferring the FX to
one of China’s many sovereign wealth funds
(including those controlled by SAFE) or the policy
banks, it should say so.
But SAFE cannot say what you see is what we really
have if it cannot explain how it handles the interest
on its now $3.5 trillion in reserves.
3. The accounting for the PBOC’s entrusted loans
and other foreign currency claims on domestic
financial institutions, notably the policy banks

FX handed over to a sovereign wealth fund to manage
doesn’t appear in reserves. It isn’t a liquid reserve asset.
But it should appear elsewhere in the balance of
payments as either portfolio debt or portfolio equity.
And FX handed over to the policy banks through
mechanisms such as entrusted loans (or a simple
deposit or swap) doesn’t count as a foreign reserve—
the foreign currency claim is on a domestic financial
institution, and that isn’t considered a “foreign” asset.

There is no doubt that the PBOC has domestic
claims on China’s financial institutions. The use of
“entrusted” loans to fund the policy banks in 2010
was reported by Caixin and the Financial Times.

### Page 9

9
$95 billion in entrusted loans were converted into
equity in the CDB and China Exim back in 2015
(giving SAFE majority equity stakes in both
institutions through its Buttonwood investment
vehicle).
And the SAFE’s 2020 annual report contained a ten-
year progress report on the “diversified use” of China’s
FX (basically, the non-reserve use) which indicated
that SAFE continued to provide entrusted loans to
small, medium, and large Chinese financial

institutions.
Now perhaps these instruments have been unwound
—the China Development Corporation (per Stephen
Green, a friend) has reduced its foreign currency
balance sheet over the last few years. But that should
lead the foreign currency to flow back to SAFE
absent another use—and that increase in FX isn’t
showing up in the PBOC’s reserves.
In other words, SAFE should provide a transparent
accounting of the FX that has been bought but
transferred to other financial institutions and any

ongoing foreign currency exposure to the domestic
banking system (including the policy banks).
These aren’t big asks. The settlement gap is big and
obvious, interest income is a known issue, and SAFE
itself has reported its use of entrusted loans in the
past. There is no issue around the past use of swaps

### Page 10

10
either. A substantial share of the banks’ foreign
currency assets are funded domestically. It should be
possible for SAFE to explain the role, if any, it has
played in this funding.
There is, of course, another issue around China’s
balance of payments: the growth in foreign currency
external assets funded out of domestic foreign
currency deposits.
China argues that the growing foreign assets of its
state banks are simply a natural part of a growing

domestic financial system. But there is more to the
story.
Onshore FX deposits have tended to rise when
economics say they should not, and fall when the
economics say they should (offshore FX deposits
behave as expected). In 2020 and 2021, FX deposits

### Page 11

11
rose even when there was more yield on RMB
deposits.
And RMB deposits have continued to rise in 2025
and 2026 even with the RMB’s appreciation
offsetting the higher dollar deposit rate. This isn’t an
accident: banks just raised the interest rate that they
pay on dollar deposits to encourage exporters NOT to
convert their export proceeds into RMB and thus
take pressure off the PBOC (per Reuters).
In other words, the pattern of the banks’ foreign

currency asset growth suggests that they are offsetting
appreciation pressure by bidding for FX (or receiving
it from other state actors), not that FX flows into the
banks during periods when onshore residents worry
about depreciation.

### Page 12

12
Right now, the “settlement gap” is the most pressing
issue. FX deposit growth in excess of FX loan growth
can explain FX outflows of around $150 billion in the
four quarters to Q3. The FX outflows implied by
settlement reached $600 billion over the last four
quarters. Together they account for the bulk of
China’s reported $825 billion plus (I estimate a Q4
2025 to Q3 2026 surplus of around $900 billion) in
outflows implied by the reported current account.***

### Page 13

13
It goes without saying that this matters because the
weak RMB is the central reason for China’s massive
export outperformance since 2023—and that export
outperformance has continued in 2026.****
* I have been doing this a long time; I actually do
know most of the historical quirks in China’s balance

### Page 14

14
of payments data.
** The MoF can count a portion of its unrepatriated
gains from accumulated interest on its large reserves
against the budget deficit, but since the actual
interest is held abroad, it still needs to borrow
domestically against those reserves (domestic JGB
issuance and foreign reserves both go up).
*** My estimate of a larger “true” current account
comes from moving gold imports over to the financial
account, and from an assumption that undercounting

of the reinvested earnings of funds held offshore
explains the obvious problem in the investment
income account.
**** The spike in imports in Q1 wasn’t entirely real.
China was slow to adjust its import price index for
the rise in semiconductor/chip prices. This inflated
both import and export volumes in Q1. The rise in
gold imports this year also registers in the import
volume series—though the rise is best understood as
“importing” a financial asset.

### Page 15

15

nonpartisan membership organization, think tank, and
publisher, and takes no institutional positions on matters
of policy.

Document 452: Top of Mind - Higher Bond Yields_ How Concerning_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Top of Mind - Higher Bond Yields_ How Concerning_.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs (Top of Mind)
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 31
Status: ok

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Global bond yields have risen to multi-decade highs, yet growth and markets have
remained remarkably resilient. But with some pressures beginning to emerge (e.g. in
European sovereign bond markets), can this resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities
be revealed? We ask the IMF’s Tobias Adrian, Moody’s Analytics’ Mark Zandi, and GS
economists. Adrian is relatively sanguine about the economy’s resilience, though
notes downside risks, while GS economists expect only a modest growth drag from

higher rates, but a more consequential one if yields remain near current or higher
levels. Zandi is generally more concerned, describing the US economy as vulnerable
rather than resilient, and sees one-in-three odds of the economy going off the r ails.
Meanwhile, GS strategists and analysts are not especially concerned about banks or
markets more broadly, but warn that further increases in yields or rate volatility could
test risk assets. Key to watch: whether the increasingly interconnected and leveraged

AI investment boom can continue to withstand higher rates.
Tobias Adrian, Special Advisor and former Director of Monetary and Capital Markets, IMF
“The global economy has demonstrated considerable resilience in the face of multiple severe shocks, and I would not be
surprised if it continues to shrug off higher yields, pr ovided they do not keep rising sharply in a disorderly fashion. At the same
time, I do worry about downside risks.”
Mark Zandi, Chief Economist, Moody’s Analytics

ISSUE 152 | October 13, 2026 | 6:10 PM EDT
HIGHER BOND YIELDS: HOW CONCERNING?
INTERVIEWS with…
ALSO inside…

A modest US growth drag  |  Pierfrancesco Mei, GS US Economics Research

We provide a brief snapshot on the most important economies for the global markets

### Page 3

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 3 
Top of Mind Issue 152

Recent acceleration in the rise in global bond yields to multi-
decade highs has begun to raise questions about the 
implications for growth and markets. Both have remained 
remarkably resilient so far, with US growth in particular 
holding relatively firm and the S&P 500 and other equity 
markets near new all-time highs. But with pressure 
beginning to emerge—most notably in European sovereign 
bond markets—and yields looking unlikely to retreat soon, 
can that resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities be revealed? 
GS Head of US Rates Strategy William Marshall first lays out 
how we arrived at higher rates, underscoring the striking 
breadth of the rise, which he attributes mainly to 
fundamentals—strong growth, inflation risks, hawkish policy 
shifts, the ongoing investment cycle, and long-run fiscal 
risks—but notes that technical factors likely amplified the 
recent move. And while he ultimately expects yields to fall 
(with YE 10-year US Treasury yields of 4.75%/4.4% in 
2026/2027, respectively) as markets become more confident 
in easing inflation, he thinks elevated long-term yields should 
have some staying power, and sees continued upside risk 
from energy price and fiscal pressures.  
So, just how much of a threat do higher rates pose to the 
macro outlook? For answers, we first turn to Tobias Adrian, 
Special Advisor to the Managing Director and former 
Financial Counsellor and Director of the Monetary and Capital 
Markets Department at the IMF, Mark Zandi, Chief 
Economist of Moody’s Analytics, and GS US economists. 
Adrian sees a global economy that has so far responded to 
the yield shock much as it has to the many other major 
shocks of the past seven years: resiliently. He notes that the 
rise in yields has not significantly tightened financial 
conditions or, more importantly, disrupted market functioning 
or forced damaging deleveraging. He believes that resilience 
could persist as long as the move in yields doesn’t accelerate 
or become more disorderly. But he is concerned about 
vulnerabilities in the AI and technology complex, sovereign 
bond markets, and EMs—not to mention cyber risks—all of 
which leaves him “vigilant but cautiously optimistic.” 
GS US economists also characterize the US economy as 
resilient. Their expectation that the Fed will deliver just one 
more rate hike this year before cutting next year—consistent 
with Marshall’s expectation of lower Treasury yields—leads 
Pierfrancesco Mei to expect only a modest 0.2pp growth 
drag in 2027 from higher rates. But he finds that the drag 
could rise to a more consequential 0.5pp if yields remain near 
current levels (see pg. 9 for GS global economists Joseph 
Briggs’ and Johan Allen’s rules of thumb for the impact of 
higher yields on rate-sensitive areas across major DMs.) 
David Mericle and Mei also worry about the implications for 
the already-concerning US fiscal trajectory, which they 
believe is unlikely to trigger a classic debt crisis but is too risky 
to ignore.     
Zandi, for his part, is generally more concerned. While his 
baseline outlook is relatively sanguine, he describes the US 
economy as vulnerable rather than resilient and views the 
“distribution of possible outcomes as very flat and heavily 
skewed toward darker tail scenarios.” He sees the clearest 
softness in the labor market, with weak hiring and falling real 
wage growth for many workers weighing on consumer 
spending. And he takes little comfort from US growth 
continuing to run around 2%—roughly its potential rate—
noting that AI accounts for roughly one-third of that growth, 
leaving the non-AI economy growing at only around 1-1.5%, 
which he calls “a pretty punk economy.” Zandi also worries 
that the US fiscal situation is “as bad as I have ever seen it” 
and that higher rates are already inflicting damage on real 
estate markets and squeezing existing debtors. As these 
stresses mount, he sees elevated risk of something breaking 
in the financial system. All told, he puts the odds of the 
current vulnerabilities pushing the economy completely off 
the rails at around one-in-three.  
With banks historically at the center of the rate shocks and 
economic crises that Zandi is worried about, we then explore 
how much risk banks pose today. Adrian and GS Head of the 
Financials Equity Group Richard Ramsden both see little 
cause for alarm, explaining that higher rates should boost 
banks’ net interest income and revenues given their asset-
sensitive balance sheets. Ramsden also doesn’t expect a 
significant deterioration in credit quality absent a broader 
economic downturn and says that most banks should have 
excess capital even after accounting for rate-driven portfolio 
losses.   
But even if banks can weather higher rates, what about 
markets more broadly? GS strategists and analysts are not 
especially concerned, with Chief US Equity Strategist Ben 
Snider arguing that current rate levels shouldn’t prevent 
continued strong US earnings growth and the S&P 500 from 
climbing higher, Chief Credit Strategist Amanda Lynam 
viewing the headwind to corporate credit from higher rates 
as manageable amid the solid growth backdrop, and US 
Financials Analyst Alexander Blostein expecting the recent 
slowdown in private market activity to rebound once rates 
stabilize. GS multi-asset strategists Christian Mueller-
Glissmann and Andrea Ferrario add that higher yields have 
strengthened the strategic case for bonds in a multi-asset 
portfolio, though strong earnings continue to favor equities 
for now. But they all warn that if rate volatility remains 
elevated, and especially if yields continue to rise from here, 
risk assets could face mounting pressures.  
All that said, with the AI boom powering the economy and 
markets, everything may come down to whether higher rates 
will derail it. Snider views this as unlikely given the strong 
balance sheets of the firms investing in AI and the expected 
profitability of these investments, with Lynam also seeing a 
high bar to derail AI-related debt issuance. Adrian also 
maintains a positive baseline for the AI economy but is 
concerned about the circularity of investment, increased 
leverage, and the sustainability of revenue growth in the AI 
complex. And Zandi again seems the most worried, warning 
that “AI could become the part of the economy that breaks” 
if rates remain at current levels for much longer.  
Allison Nathan, Editor

Higher bond yields: how concerning?
